CommentarytoBasicSentences
10.1 Iday
Iday isatermofaddressforgirlsof
thesame ageoryoungerthanspeakeror
isused as anickname forgirls.
10.3
Nápalitgad
laakhin..
Literalythismeans 'Iamjustbuying
onlyearings.
' Shesays'onlyear-
rings'meaning she cannotbuyanyof
theotherthingsthesalesgirlwas
tryingtoselher.
10.6 ngáhaw
Theuse ofngáhawinthisstatement is
trigeredbythesalesgirl'sclaimin
===== PAGE 24 =====
Questions onBasic Sentences 10-7
thebeginning thathergoods were
inexpensive. When thebuyerasksfor
theearings' price, thesalesgirl
sortofapologizesforthehighprice
byusingngahawandgoes ontoexplain
whytheearingsareratherexpensive.
10.6 gu
Insome dialectsthedofgud, gad,
lugud,sugad, andotherparticlesis
dropped.
10.14 kunu'They say,hesays! Kunuisused to
indicate indirectquotations.

formationandmeaning ofwhichwilbe
takenupinLesson13.

10.2 Subjectmarkersanandit
WaraydifersfromEnglish inthatnotonlyverbs,
alsootherpartsofthesentence indicate time. Thesubject,
forexample, oftenindicates time. The subjectmarkerit
(shortforitun)has twouses: (1)Itindicates time: it
marks a subjectand indicatesthatthissubjectisatthepresent
orinthefuturetime; (2) itdoes not indicate timebut
marks asubjectand indicates thatthesubjectisunspecified.
Anhastwouses. (1)Anmarks asubjectand indicates itis
past time; (2)andoes not indicate time, but marks asub-
jectthatisspecified. Themake-upofthesubjectandpre-
dicate (thepartofspeechofthesubjectandthepartsof
speechofthepredicate) indicateswhetheritandanhave
meanings (1) or(2). Thisdiference isexplainedinthe
folowingsubsections.
10.21 Caseswhere itreferstopresent andfuturetimeand

11.1 Potentialformsofthedirectpassiveverb
Likeactiveverbsthepassive verbshavepotentialforms.
Thefolowingchartshows thepotentialformsofthedirect
passive verbs:
Past Present Future Dependent PastSubjuntive FutureSubj.
na- na-R- ma--R- ma- ma-,ka- ma
-R-, ka-R-
Thepresenttensepotentialgivesthemeaning'canbe[done]';
thepasttense, 'succeeded inbeing [done]';thefuturetense,
'wilbeabletobe[done].
'
Nahuman munabaanbáduq?'Haveyoufinished(hasbeen
finishedbyyou)mydressyet?' [pasttense]
Nahuhumannaníyaanbáduq pagkadtukuhafra.
justfinishingupthedresswhenIwenttothehouse.

"

caryheavy things.

11.2 Meaningofthepotentialconjugation

11.4 Secondaryafix, -in-(S)*
Afixeswhichareadded toverbsandwhichindicate time
(present,past, future, potential, etc.afixes) arecaled
PRIMARY AFFIXES, e.g.gin-inginkítaq (wasseen) isaprimary
afixbecauseitindicates pasttense:
Ginkitaqkuhiya.
'Isawhim.
'
Similarly-un, R-
un, etc.,mag-, mag-R, etc.alareprimary
afixesbecausetheyalindicatetense.
Otherafixesare
addedtoverbs butdonotindicate tense. Theseafixesare
caledSECONDARY AFFIXES. The primary afixesindicatingtense
areadded toverbswhichmayalreadycontainsecondaryafixes.
Thus,forexample inginpangúhaq, theprefixpar-isasecond-
aryafixwhichwasadded tokúhaq'obtained' formingpangúhaq
'obtained (plural).
' Theprimary afixgin-isaddedto
pangúhaq toformginpangúhaq wasobtained (plural)':
'was
Ginpangúhaqníyaanmgabutang.
'He gotthethings.
Afrequently occuringsecondaryafixistheinfix
-in-(—)*
. Itforms
Itformsaverbwhichreferstoanactionwhich
'
lastedalongtime. Theinfixisplacedimmediately folowing
thefirstconsonantoftheroot, oriftherootbeginswitha
vowel, immediately preceding thefirstvowel.
Verbscontain-
ingthesecondaryafix-in-alwayshave themag-conjugation
fortheactiveforms.

XIVE. FreeConversation
Pretendyou have justbought some earingstogiveaway
asapresent toafriendofyours. Youshowthemtoyourroommate.

GrammaticalSection 14-17
Shesaysthey're pretyandasksyouwhere youbought them.
You
telher. Thenyouaskhertoput themonsoyou canseehow
theylook. Shedoesand youbothadmire theearings. Your
roommate decides shewants tobuysome earings, too.
You
telherthatifshe wantstobuy themnow, you'd betergo
rightawaybecause there's verylitletimeleft.
Thestore
won'tbeopen much longer. Yourfriendsaysshe'sreadyto
go rightnow. Thenyouremember thepieceofclothyouwant
totaketotheseamstress's. Thiswouldbeagood time todrop
itatherhouse onthewaytothestore. Youaskyour roommate
towaitamoment whileyougetyourpieceofcloth ready.
Yourroommate ofersyou some papertowrapyourdress material
with.
Thenasyou leavethehouse youaskherifshe has any
money topaythefarewith. Shesaysshe doesn't.
You sayyou
don'teither. Youonlyhave afive-peso bilandyouaskif
thejeepdriverwouldbe abletochange it. Yourroommate
saysshe thinkshecan, butincase he doesn'tperhapsit
wouldbebetertogetsome change fromthepiggybank.
You
sayyouhatetobreakit. Yourroommate saysshe knows how
togetmoney outofthebank withoutbreaking it.
GrammaticalSection
14.1 TheAblative Passive
InLesson8and9we studiedthedirectpassive.
In
Lesson13we studiedthelocalpassive.
Inthislessonwe
===== PAGE 255 =====
14-18 Lesson14
studytheABLATIVE passive. Thefolowingchartshowsthe
formsoftheablative passive:
Past Present Future Dependent Imperative
igin- igin--R- i--R-, ig -R- i- i-,ig

14.2 Ablative passive expressing conveyance
Theablative passive formofverbsmeaning'give, pay,
put, throw, sel,
' andthelikeexpresses theconveyance mean-
ing. Withthistypeofverbthe recipient referstothething
conveyed fromone pointtoanother. Insentenceswithablative
passives intheconveyance meaning thefocusisthething
acteddirectlyupon (that is, conveyed). (Thisconstruction
usualyoccurswithverbs thathave nodirectpassive forms,
butnotalways-seeLesson15,below.)

XVIE. FreeConversation
Pretend you areMinda,apublicschoolteacher.
Have
oneofyourclassmates playthe roleofthemaid. Youcome

16.1 Theprefixpa-
Theprefixpa-maybe addedtoany rootwhichisaverb.
Themeaning ofpa-is'cause someonetodotheactionofthe
verb.
'* Lútuqmeans'cook'; palútug means'have someone,
'
letsomeone, ormake someone cook.

cooksome fish.

orletsomeonepay.

'Itisnotgood foryou tomakeme payextra.
'

verbs. Itisadded tosome adjectives toformverbsmeaning
'make [adjective]':e.g.,mahúsay'beautiful'; pahúsay'make
beautiful.

thistime. Youquotea price,andwhensheexclaims about itbeing
toohigh, you immediately telhereverything isexpensive nowadays.

17.1 Existentialsentences and indefinitepronouns
InSec. 5.2wediscussed existentialsentences. Thissection
brieflyreviews what welearned there. AnEXISTENTIAL SENTENCE is
asentencewhichafirms ordenies theexistence ofsomething orwhich
says thatsomebodyhas ordoesn'thave [such-and-sucha thing]:
May kukutsi.
'Ihave acar.
'
Waray kukutsi.
'Ihave no car.
'
Aadihinkaranqun.
'Herearesome cookies.
'
Existentialsentences areused alsotoexpresstheconcepts
expressedbytheEnglish INDEFINITE PRONOUNS.

arethewords'somebody,
